     conference'' means a conference the membership of which 
     consists predominantly of part B institutions.
       (6) Intercollegiate athletic association; conference.--The 
     terms ``intercollegiate athletic association'' and 
     ``conference'' have the meanings given those terms in section 
     100 of this Act.
       (7) Part b institution.--The term ``Part B institution'' 
     has the meaning given the term

[[Page S5063]]

     in section 322 of the Higher Education Act of 1965 (20 U.S.C. 
     1061).
       (8) Secretary.--The term ``Secretary'' means the Secretary 
     of Education.
       (9) State.--The term ``State'' has the meaning given the 
     term in section 103 of the Higher Education Act of 1965 (20 
     U.S.C. 1003).

     SEC. 307. AUTHORIZATION OF APPROPRIATIONS.

       There is authorized to be appropriated to carry out this 
     title $180,000,000 for each of fiscal years 2027 through 
     2032.
  The PRESIDING OFFICER. Under the previous order, the motion to 
reconsider is considered made and laid upon the table.
  The Senator from Washington.


                   Protect College Sports Act of 2026

  Ms. CANTWELL. Mr. President, I rise to clarify that the effect of the 
antitrust exemption in section 118 of the Protect College Sports Act in 
the event that the House settlement expires or terminates and Congress 
does not extend the revenue share cap.
  To be clear, the antitrust immunity for enforcing and complying with 
sections 114 and 115 applies only to the extent that the provisions of 
those sections continue to have force or effect.
  Senator Coons, do you agree that this is the intent of the 
legislation?
  Mr. COONS. Senator Cantwell, I agree that that is the intent of our 
legislation.
  Ms. CANTWELL. Mr. President, I note that the Protect College Sports 
Act is also critical to local broadcasters.
  Right now, too many college football games are trapped behind 
paywalls. The Protect College Sports Act aims to change that by 
supporting consumers' access to TV.
  And I understand Senator Moran has entered a statement in the record 
on this topic; and I agree with him on this, in putting college 
football on free TV on local broadcasting stations instead of in 
paywalls.
  I did not, before the vote, take time to thank all the people 
involved with this legislation, and I would like to take that 
opportunity now.
  First of all, I want to thank Senator Cruz for his partnership on 
this legislation and working to create a bipartisan bill that I think 
the majority of our colleagues showed tonight that they support, and I 
hope that we will get our colleagues in the House of Representatives to 
also agree on that before the end of the year.
  Obviously, this legislative process is a challenging one, and we 
would not have been here at this moment without a lot of help from a 
lot of different people.
  I want to thank especially Senators Schmitt and Coons for their 
tremendous leadership in being partners with Senator Cruz and I on this 
legislation. The two of them provided key input, support--debatable, 
argumentative points of view at various points of time. But there is no 
way we would have gotten this legislation over the goal line without 
Senator Schmitt and Senator Coons, and I just want to thank them 
immensely for their long partnership in working with Senator Cruz and 
myself.
  And I also want to thank James Barton, who is with Senator Coons, 
because he played a key role in all of this, and I very much appreciate 
his support. I also want to thank key members of the Commerce Committee 
because they played very important roles in this: Senator Hickenlooper 
drafting the language and negotiation as it related to the U.S. Olympic 
provisions of the bill; Senator Lujan for drafting and getting 
amendments accepted related to the coaches; Senator Baldwin on a key 
amendment that we just adopted tonight by, as I said earlier, a record 
96 to 1 vote, which shows that this place, when you let the right 
people in the room, can come to a conclusion--not just that, but that 
our colleagues also trust that that process can work too and rewarded 
that with a big, big vote tonight. So very much appreciate that.
  I also appreciate the President's sports council--Randy Levine, Cody 
Campbell, Jack Swarbrick--all played big roles in this, probably nobody 
more than Randy Levine in constantly pushing the White House point of 
view on this legislation and Cody Campbell making sure that everybody 
understood that opening up the Sports Broadcasting Act was a great 
idea. That also seconded, obviously, by Jack, who added an AD 
perspective to the debate.
  I also want to thank a couple of big Washingtonians who played a key 
role in this. Mark Few, the famous--now Hall of Fame--Gonzaga coach who 
was just constant--now I know what it would have been like to play for 
Gonzaga because he was constantly saying: Maria, it is just about 
getting the basics done, about eligibility, about transfer, and about 
basic rules. And he reminded me of that all the time. I also want to 
thank Washington AD Pat Chun, who took endless phone calls from us.
  I want to thank WSU President Cantwell, who was in a trying situation 
of her own. Literally, the Pac-12 blew up, took $35 million of revenue 
out of her institution, and she had to figure out a path forward and 
engage on this legislation and try to help us all think about what is a 
fair way to move college sports forward, and she did that. And I 
appreciate her time.
  And certainly Pac-12 commissioner Teresa Gould, who testified before 
the committee and added a lot of input about what not to do next, and I 
just can't thank her enough because I see even tonight some of this 
debate veered into the realm of what not to do.
  And I hope that we can continue to make corrections on that as we 
dialogue with our House colleagues because the last thing that we need 
to do is have another Pac-12 incident. But we need to grow regional 
sports, grow the regional system, and make it stronger.

  I also want to thank the many people on the Commerce Committee staff 
who--oh, I have to thank Senator Welch. Senator Welch also signing on 
early to the legislation, Senator Peters helping us with the Big 10, 
Senator Klobuchar doing the same, all of them were so helpful in this 
legislation.
  Now to the committee staff. Senator Cruz talked a lot about how this 
negotiation went on for months and months and months and hours and 
hours a day, and he talked about Cuban coffee. All that was true.
  It took a lot to keep us in a room discussing immense legal terms 
with a variety of conferences and individuals and athletes from all 
over the country. But I want to thank staff director Lila Helms, our 
general counsel Melissa Porter, Nicole Frazer, Phil Murphy, and Ramesh 
Nagarajan. Those individuals right there spent the majority of time 
working on this legislation.
  But Shannon Smith, who served on the committee for 5 years and 
recently retired, she deserves a jersey all by herself. I kid with her 
about saying it is going to say ``Jaxon Smith-Njigba,'' but instead, it 
is just going to say ``Shannon Smith'' and then some name because she 
deserves a new title for the immense amount of effort. We would just 
not even have a bill on the floor tonight if it weren't for her 
efforts.
  But Nicole and Melissa and Lila, I can't say enough about their 
leadership and patience in driving a process that was just fraught with 
different opinions. That is just all there is to it, fraught with 
different opinions and a lot of negotiations.
  So I think it will go down in history as one of the most challenging 
issues because, unlike a lot of policy issues that we deal with here in 
the U.S. Senate, you have different issues and they follow a certain 
path. They follow a construct. It might be consumer versus business; it 
might be various things. In this case, we had a lot of different 
viewpoints across the entire ecosystem of sports. And if there was a 
characterization here, it was definitely that nobody wanted the two big 
divisions to run away and be a super league and continue to X out the 
revenue and that everybody wanted to drive some sanity back into the 
system.
  But that sounds simple compared to what these individuals went 
through. So I really can't thank enough Lila Helms, Melissa Porter, and 
Nicole Frazer for their incredible work on this. Phil and Ramesh, great 
work, challenging policy, appreciate their efforts.
  I want to thank Elizabeth Cuttner; Erik Eykel; Kate Sprague; Connor 
Jennings; Tricia Enright; Rob Blumenthal; Ciara O'Donoghue; Josephine 
Laing; Zoe Garrett; and Amit Ronen from our staff, who is here on the 
floor tonight; Ansley Lacitis, who I think got to know the entire press 
corps for sports across the United States and took all their questions 
and probably has some to feed back to them; Lauren Bazel; Seth Kolloen.

[[Page S5064]]

  And I want to thank Senator Cruz' staff because, obviously, they 
worked very hard on this legislation. Brad Grantz, his staff director; 
Nicole Christus; Joel Wellum--I want to thank Joel specifically because 
Joel obviously took a lot of direct questioning, a lot of pushing by us 
and me particularly, and I appreciate his hard work--Michael Spierto; 
Ryan Cannon; and Justin Adams and Casey Contres.
  So there is no way to really sum up all of the thanks in one 
statement. I do want to thank my colleague Senator Moran, who is on the 
floor tonight, because he started this effort in the committee many 
years ago and had great thoughts on what we needed to do and got us all 
pointed in the right direction and kept the dialogue moving through 
myself and Senator Wicker, and then as Senators Booker and Blumenthal 
and I continued to dialogue, and then ultimately Senator Schmitt and I, 
and Senator Coons and Schmitt and I, and then ultimately this 
legislation with Senator Cruz and I. So this long, challenged situation 
of figuring out how to make more sense out of college sports, I want to 
thank him as well.
  So we definitely made the will of the Senate work tonight, and I hope 
we can do more of that. I hope we can find where there is the ability 
to solve problems. We obviously have a very big difference of opinion, 
but there probably isn't a bigger gulf and difference of opinion.
  I say Senator Cruz and I probably disagree on 25 different issues, 
and that is just in the Commerce Committee, but if we can do that 
tonight and produce a product, I guarantee you the rest of us can.
  And tonight, it is about making sports about sports again. So I thank 
my colleagues for this legislation and now on to working with our 
colleagues in the House.
  I yield the floor.
  The PRESIDING OFFICER. The Senator from Delaware.
  Mr. COONS. Mr. President, I just wanted to briefly add my comments 
this evening to thank Senator Cantwell and Senator Cruz. They have 
shown how two Senators from very different backgrounds, different 
regions, and different political values can work together tirelessly to 
hammer out a bipartisan solution that tonight won 77 votes on the floor 
of the U.S. Senate.
  I want to specifically thank my chief counsel James Barton, who 
helped me get up to speed on this issue and make modest contributions 
at the early stages of this legislation, and to thank the staff of 
Senators Cantwell and Cruz for their tireless work on this over many 
cups of Cuban coffee, over many late-night conversations, over lots of 
texts and emails and meetings.
  It was my honor to make small contributions to this great work by 
Senators Cantwell and Cruz, who have demonstrated what is possible in 
this body when folks bend their will toward something that matters to 
hundreds of millions of Americans but that is complicated and has lots 
of competing interests and lots of challenging issues at stake.
  I look forward to working with my friends and colleagues in the House 
to ensure this bill gets passed and signed into law.
  I yield the floor.
  The PRESIDING OFFICER (Mr. Moreno). The Senator from Kansas.
